# LOG archive, 2026-11

Entries moved out of LOG.md by scripts/archive_logs.py to keep the living
file small enough to read cheaply. Nothing here is edited or summarised: it is
the original text, newest first. LOG.md links back to this file.

## 2026-11-20 reader judge: the Curtain Square fig's first photographs, and its pin is now where the fig stands

- **mel_005, The Moreton Bay Fig of Curtain Square**: two readers photographed the fig itself at the Canning Street side of the square. db51a305 is the lead photograph. 72e483a0 was sent for the plane (mel_006) but shows the same fig 13 m away, so it went into the strip. The pin moved 110 m to the photograph's GPS fix and is confirmed again; this settles the run above's "approximate until a photograph of the fig places it". The readers' mails are pending in the weekly bundle.
- **mel_006, The London Plane**: its register pin (City of Yarra ref 190) is where that fig stands, so it is now `approximate` with a verify_note until somebody photographs the plane. The register's two Curtain Square points do not match the trees on the ground (the fig point is the elm mel_021).
- This run started at the same time as the run above and duplicated its trees. That commit was dropped before it was pushed (branch `backup-reader-judge-2026-11-20-dup`, local only), and only the fig work that run had not done was redone. The two runs disagreed on one tree: the other run held the Cáceres sweet gum (6991cd88) as `not_special`, and this run would have published it for its colour. The hold stands; it is a judgement call Hidde can overturn.

## 2026-11-20 reader judge: two Bristol oaks, a canal willow in Ottawa, and the Curtain Square "fig" that is an elm

- **Live, readers' trees**: bri_024 The Split Oak on the Stoke Park Slope and bri_025 The Antlered Oak (Bristol, now 25 trees), ott_022 The Thick-trunked Willow of the Rideau Canal (Ottawa, now 22), all with the photograph's own GPS (confirmed) and month (10, 11, 9). bri_025's access line says we have not confirmed who owns the wood. The willow is `Willow (Salix sp.)`; the reader's "crack willow" is in the story as theirs. Tip 1333 was the same Stoke Park oak sent twice. Place counts updated (Bristol intro and meta, Ottawa meta, context, FAQ).
- **mel_021 The Furrowed Giant of Curtain Square** (Melbourne, now 21): three photographs from two readers, all sent for our Moreton Bay fig mel_005 from within 15 m of its pin, all show the same furrowed-barked elm with small toothed leaves and no buttress roots. Verdict `other_tree`, then live as its own tree, species `Elm (Ulmus sp.)` with an ask, lead plus two in the strip. Pin approximate: the leads carry no `location_kind`. Melbourne intro and walk FAQ now say 21 and nine in the Carlton North walk.
- **Something published is wrong, probably**: mel_005's pin is the City of Yarra register's coordinate and it lands on that elm, not a fig. Set `approximate` with a verify_note; the pin stays until a photograph of the fig places it. A session could check whether the register itself is right about a fig in Curtain Square.
- **Held**: 6991cd88 (Cáceres), a slender young sweetgum in autumn colour among similar trees, `not_special`, so no mail.
- **Side fix**: nyc_038 `Weeping Willow (Salix sp.)` became `Willow (Salix sp.)`, the one name every other Salix sp. uses (hard rule 9, preflight).
- Mail reply 1339: a reader will send a California redwood in a few weeks; closed `handled`. 0 open reports. Congratulation mails for the four new trees go once they are in the live feed (tree_approved.py on the next knock). **Not judged**: 3c544597 (our own account), still no file.

## 2026-11-20 session: the map walk

- **Every web map now walks the same steps before a deploy goes live** (Hidde: "welke vraag moet ik jou stellen dat je overal dezelfde kaart ervaring goed fixed"). scripts/pagecheck.py, already run in Chrome before the upload, walks the city, explore, country and state maps at 1440 and 1024 wide: pins stand absolute, the same buttons (zoom, one find-me, no full screen), pointing at a pin or a list row lights it and moves no other pin, a click opens the card beside its pin with its photograph, a full map keeps its place in the address. ## 2026-11-20 session: the Android tester group said "no permission" to everyone

- **Breakage, found by a tester** (journalist reply to wave 3: "it says I don't have permission"): the Google Group was set to "who can view conversations: owners only", so its link in the wave-3 mail showed non-members "You don't have permission to access this content" instead of the group. About 266 of the 277 wave-3 mails sent today pointed there (the first 11 are also on Alpha's e-mail list). The group had 12 members.
- **Fixed in Chrome on Hidde's word**: who can view conversations is now "anyone on the web" (posting stays owners only, members list managers only, anyone can join). Seen signed out: the page now reads "No conversations yet" instead of the error. Google shows the Join button only to someone signed in, which every tester is (a Gmail address). Not seen as a signed-in non-member.
- **Tested by Hidde** with a second Google account: join works.
- **Added by hand**: 37 of wave 3 straight into the group ("Leden rechtstreeks toevoegen"). Google takes at most 10 per round and asks a captcha every round, so after four rounds Hidde stopped it ("dit schiet niet op"). Not a route for later waves.
- **SENT on Hidde's "Verstuur"**: a follow-up to the 262 wave-3 recipients not yet in the group, through Resend (batch android-test-invite-wave3-fixup-2026-11-20, gitignored): the 37 we added got the Play link only, 225 got both steps. All 262 sent, none held. Group at 54 members at send time.
- **Open**: the 500 of wave 3 still waiting can go with the existing text, each day on his word.

## 2026-11-20 session: sign-in mail was down for everyone; Resend Pro brings it back

- **Breakage**: from about 12:00 UTC no email sign-in link could go out. His Gmail (the account behind info@) hit its daily limit during wave 3, and Resend's free 100 were used up by the overflow; the hook tries both and both refused. Tested at 14:40 UTC: Gmail still answered "550 5.4.5 Daily user sending limit exceeded". Google and Apple sign-in kept working. Failed requests leave probably no trace in Supabase, so signin_watch.py cannot catch those people up; they get in when they try again.
- **Fixed (Hidde: "Yes, Pro", upgraded himself)**: Resend Pro, 50,000 a month, no daily cap. A sign-in request for apptest@ went through right after (hook answered OK). `RESEND_DAY` 1600 and `RESEND_FOR_STRANGERS` 1000 in mailer.py; test_mailer.py green.
- **Still true**: his personal Gmail cannot send until Google lifts the block (within 24 hours of about 12:00 UTC). No wave 3 portion until then.
- **Live since about 15:10 UTC (send-email v11, Hidde: "for now yes")**: every sign-in and welcome mail goes through Resend first, Gmail only as backup, so a mail wave on Gmail can no longer block sign-in. Switch: `RESEND_FIRST` in the hook. Tested: a sign-in request for apptest@ went through v11 while Gmail was still blocked, so through Resend.
- **Not done**: the website's "try again in a minute" on a failed send is untrue in an outage like this one, and typed in English only.

## 2026-11-20 session: pins stacked and jumped on every web map (found by Hidde, fixed)

Every pin then sat lower by the height of the pins before it in the page, so the pins jumped whenever one came or went (pointing along the list, a tap) and a chosen pin stood far from its own card (Hidde on Kyoto: "als ik ... hover over de lijst dan verspringen de pins en de pins openen niet de airbnb preview"). My test page hid it because the live style.css still carried the old rule then. Fixed: no position on .pin-tree; qa `check_every_map_shows_pins_the_same_way()` refuses one in pinCss() or style.css (tested both ways); escaped bug recorded with that check. And pointing at a tree in the list no longer moves the other pins at all (it was full-pin-first, so neighbours could turn into dots): eight hovers on Kyoto, zero pin moved; a tapped dot's pin now stands beside its card.

## 2026-11-20 session: crashes are now found and dived into without asking

**What changed.** Hidde: "we need a system in place where if you find about crashes you dive into them i shouldnt have to ask you". scripts/app_crashes.py reads the crashes the app reports itself to PostHog (both platforms: `app_exit_abnormal`, `app_crash_note`, `$exception`), strangers only, with what each phone did in the ten minutes before. health.py rings on any crash of 48 hours that has no answer in data/crash-fixes.json, so it is rung-2 work for the night run (POSTHOG_READ_KEY now in its three agent steps). Until today only the iPhone's MetricKit table was read, so eleven Android crashes sat unread.

**Android 4 (not built for Play yet):** a crash note. ExitWatch.install writes the exception types and our top code lines to the phone at the crash, the next launch sends it as `trace` and deletes it; R8 keeps our class names so it reads without the mapping file. Proven on the emulator end to end: a forced crash arrived in PostHog with its trace. Unit tests in ExitWatchTest.

**The crashes.** 11 on 4 testers' phones, 11-17 to 11-19, builds 1 to 3, all "crash" with no line: PostHog's own crash handler (in since build 2) delivered none, though it works on the emulator, and Play vitals shows nothing (testers do not share diagnostics). Lead: the 11-17 phone crashed four times in a row on opening My trees right after recording a sighting; the others crashed after My trees or a collect. Not reproduced. Answered in crash-fixes.json until build 4's notes arrive.

**For the next iPhone build (42):** RELEASE_CHECKLIST.md "A crash must name its line": crash it on purpose once and see the line in app_crashes.py before release, dSYMs uploaded. 42 is the first iPhone build with PostHog's crash handler.

## 2026-11-20 session: wave 3 of the Android test goes through a Google Group on a second track

- **Changed in Play** (Hidde: "Yes, prepare it", "what ever you think works"): a Google Group, groups.google.com/g/ancienttrees-android-testers (anyone can join; members list managers only; posting and conversations owners only), is the tester list of a new closed track "Beta (Google Group)" with build 4 and all countries, saved for review and waiting for Hidde's "indienen voor beoordeling" click. Alpha is untouched: its e-mail list holds 485 (wave 3 lines 1 to 11 were added before the switch).
- **Why**: Play's tester list refuses some addresses without saying which (two in wave 3's first 50), so adding 778 by halving would take hundreds of saves. Play allows one access method per track (its help: "select your preferred tester access method"), so the group gets its own track. Later waves need no Play edits at all.
- **Status at 14:00**: Alpha's build 4 is approved and live (green in Play the same day). Beta's 7 changes submitted by Hidde, in review.
- **Breakage: Gmail's daily limit reached on 2026-11-20.** Second portion on Hidde's "verstuur 180" (my advice: 350 that day was safe). All 180 went, but not as I first reported: the log shows batch positions 1 to 182 through Gmail and then Gmail's "550 5.4.5 Daily user sending limit exceeded", after which mailer.py quietly sent the other **97 through Resend**, which with the 7 catch-ups filled Resend's 100 (Resend mailed Hidde "100% of your daily quota"). **Fixed the same hour** (f3cd1d8f6, test_mailer.py): Resend's sign-in reserve now holds for a mail that lands on Resend because Gmail refused, not only for one that wants Resend by kind; a batch that finds no route left stops with HOLD lines for the rest instead of crashing. My estimate was wrong too: the log held 451 mails dated that day (replies, wave 2's remainder, catch-ups, wave 3), and Supabase's sign-in and welcome mail through Gmail comes on top and is not in that log. Until Gmail lets info@ send again (up to 24 hours), every mail falls back to Resend (100 free a day), sign-in mail included. **Lesson**: before raising the cap, count every Gmail mail of the last 24 hours (data/outreach-sent.json plus the sign-in hook), and keep that total under about 400. Wave 3 so far: 277 sent, 500 waiting.
- **SENT, first part, on Hidde's "Verstuur"** (after Beta went green the same afternoon): 97 of wave 3 through Gmail. The day's cap of 170 counts the first-contact mails outreach_send.py sent that day (about 73 already), not replies or sign-in mail. 658 wait for the next days. 22 were first skipped because we had mailed them before; Hidde: "dont skip the 22 this is a relevant mail!", so all 22 now carry a resend_reason in the batch and go with the daily portions (one of them, mailed by hand on 11-16, waits for the week's rest until 11-23). Rule for the next waitlist mail: an earlier mail is no reason to leave someone out of a mail they signed up for. Each next portion only after asking Hidde that day (his choice: "Ask me each day"); same command: `OUTREACH_DAILY_CAP=170 python3 out/tmp/send_with_mail_env.py drafts/batches/android-test-invite-wave3-2026-11-20.json --send`.
- **Ready, not sent**: drafts/batches/android-test-invite-wave3-2026-11-20.json, 777 waitlist Gmail addresses, Hidde's text plus a "join the tester group" step; send after Beta is approved, on his "verstuur", about 170 a day (command in drafts/android-test-invite.md).
- **For Hidde**: the group's public info page shows him as owner with his Gmail address. Adding the info address as a manager does not hide the owner; only moving ownership to another account would.

Rebuilt twice so those two late commits went in; unit tests green and the Play build opened on the emulator each time.

Freezes (ANR) and map crashes carry no note, so those were lost completely. The first look now sets the mark to now.
- **Proven (RELEASE_CHECKLIST "A crash must name its line")**: minified build 4 on the emulator, clean install, `am crash`, reopen: `app_exit_abnormal` reason crash, on screen yes, with its trace, in PostHog at 09:40:58 UTC. Two traps for the next proof: the Play build sends nothing on an emulator by design (use `assembleMinified` with `--esa args -measure`), and `am crash` does not land while the location dialog is on top (`pm grant ... ACCESS_FINE_LOCATION` first).
- **Not done**: MapLibre native symbols. Its Maven .so files come stripped, so `ndk.debugSymbolLevel` put nothing in the bundle and Play's warning stays (noted in build.gradle.kts). The instrumented walks (android_prelaunch.py) did not run this time; unit tests are green.
- **Next**: wave 3, see the entry above.

## 2026-11-19 session: readers' photos filed under their season

- **What changed:** Hidde saw empty Seasons rows under a chestnut in copper leaf (Divonne-les-Bains) and cherries in blossom (Fukutsu). The reader judge writes photo blocks by hand, and its brief said only snow could set a month. 137 readers' photos outside the tropics had no `taken_month`.
- **Done:** both trees filed (autumn, spring). Three photo-judge passes looked at the other 135: 32 dated (26 autumn, 6 spring), 103 show no season and say so in `taken_month_basis`. Lines inserted only, 169 lines across 93 city files.
- **Rule:** BRIEF_READER_JUDGE.md widened (autumn colour, blossom, a send date that agrees). New qa check `check_a_reader_photo_is_filed_by_season()` refuses a reader's photo outside the tropics with neither field; verified red and green. **Narrowed 2026-11-20** (Hidde: "het mag zonder maar als de info er is moeten we het opslaan"): it now fails only when the lead knows the month and the photo lost it; a photo with no known date needs nothing. Red on castleboro/cbo_001 with its month removed in a copy, green on the real data.
- **Note:** many reader photos carry "(2026-09-04)" in their note, an import date and not the day they were sent; the passes did not use it.
- **Later, the source of the gap** (Hidde: "het gat dat de tijd niet wordt opgeslagen van input"): both apps send the photo's own date, but sightings_inbox.py dropped it from the public lead file, so hand-built trees never had it; and the website's add-photo sent no date at all (its shrink step drops EXIF), so Supabase kept the upload moment. Now: leads carry `taken_month` (month only), the queue keeps a date only when it is the photo's own (`photo_taken_at()`, tests in test_sightings.py), and the website reads DateTimeOriginal before shrinking (`atPhotoDate`, image-downsize-js.ts; tested in the browser pane with a JPEG dated 2025-07-14) and says in the row's note which date it sent. Backfill from Supabase: 91 live photos got their real month where it was at least two days older than the day we saw it; in 5 clashes (Fukutsu's cherries were taken in February, i.e. Winter) the picture's season stayed.
- **Open, needs Hidde:** the website's tip form (contribute page, `submissions` table) has no date column; adding one is a new column on readers' data (hard list 1). **2026-11-20, his yes ("Yes, add it"):** `submissions.photo_month` (1 to 12, month only), supabase/submission-photo-month.sql, privacy page and privacy map updated. The form reads each photo's month before shrinking and sends the first uploaded photo's; until the SQL is pasted it retries without the column. The inbox reads it as optional and no longer takes a tip's send moment for its photo's date (that had already put the Zetland fig under month 11 on 11-20; undone). Pasted by Hidde 2026-11-20, columns checked live.

## 2026-11-19 session: Resend keeps 40 a day for sign-in mail; stuck sign-ups get their mail again

- **Reserve** (Hidde: "ja graag"): first mails to strangers may use 60 of Resend's 100 a day (`RESEND_FOR_STRANGERS` in scripts/mailer.py, counted from the `route` outreach_send.py now logs), then go through Gmail. Yahoo, Apple-relay and BT addresses always keep Resend. Tests in scripts/test_mailer.py.
- **Watch**: `scripts/signin_watch.py --send` at every knock (nightly.yml): an email sign-up more than an hour old that never got in gets the same sign-in mail again, signed-only addresses once more after midnight UTC, never a third time, nothing after three days. State under people.ref codes in data/signin-watch.json; a line in the digest's lower part. Tests in scripts/test_signin_watch.py.
- **First run found 9 people stuck since 11-16** (none from today's Resend outage: everyone since 09:36 got in via Apple or Google). 8 got their mail again; 1 address had a typo in its domain and cannot receive mail.
- **Existing accounts too** (Hidde: "kunnen we dat nagaan?"): the auth audit log is empty on this project, but an unused link stays in auth.one_time_tokens. New function `public.stuck_signin_links()` (supabase/stuck-signin-links.sql, run through the Management API, service key only, ids only; the public key gets 401) feeds the same watch.
- **For Hidde: 31 people since the Guardian day asked for a sign-in link and never got in** (25 new accounts from 15 and 16 November, 11 of them Yahoo/Apple/BT; 6 existing accounts). On his "waarom niet nu al?": the 19 on the Gmail route got their mail again that evening (1 address cannot receive mail); the 12 Yahoo/Apple/BT ones go with the knocks between 00:05 and 18:00 UTC on 11-20, when Resend's day has started over (`CATCH_UP` in signin_watch.py, expires by itself). Check the digest line on 11-20 that they went.

## 2026-11-19 session: the Android invitation filled Resend; waitlist batches now go through Gmail

- **Breakage**: the 100 Android test invitations went through outreach_send.py, which always sent as a first mail to a stranger (Resend). Resend's free 100 a day was full by 09:36. Until midnight UTC, sign-in and welcome mail to Yahoo, Apple-relay and BT addresses falls back to unsigned Gmail and may bounce; everyone else is unaffected (Gmail is their route anyway).
- **Fix**: a batch file may say `"kind": "expected"` (people who asked to hear from us: waitlist, testers) and then goes through Gmail, Resend only for the Yahoo/Apple/BT addresses in it. The Android batch carries it, so tomorrow's 68 do not touch Resend.

## 2026-11-19 reader judge: the Kasama leaf-seed ginkgo live from a reader's tip
- **Live, new place: kasama/kas_001 The Leaf-Seed Ginkgo of Inada Gobo** (Ibaraki, Japan), from a reader's website tip with no photograph (submission 1233, `tip:1233` publish). Ibaraki Prefecture's own record (kyoiku.pref.ibaraki.jp/bunkazai/ken-160) names it: a prefectural natural monument since 2000 at Sainen-ji, 469 Inada, girth 7.5 m, height 35 m, spread 23 m, more than 300 years, vigorous; temple tradition says planted around 1218 in Shinran's time. Register plus the reader's report from this autumn as the life check. Pin approximate at the temple (OSM node), no photograph yet; the reader's girth matches the register's. One tree, so no place page on the web.
- **For a session: our Mito page (mit_001) calls the Shirahatayama tree the largest leaf-seed ginkgo known**, while Ibaraki's register gives the Inada tree the wider trunk (7.5 m against 6 m). The Kasama story states the register's comparison as the register's and claims no crown; the Mito line wants a look.
- Nothing else waited: no reader photographs in the queue, no open reports, no feedback without a verdict, no reader words to quote. Still open: 3c544597 (our own account, file not on this machine).

- **What changed in the apps** (next TestFlight build and the Android build after 0.1.0 (2)): signed-out My trees on Android opens just high enough to show Sign in at big text on a small phone (the half stop fits short content; the iPhone already opens one stop higher on small phones); Move the pin on Android takes the Android form bar (close X, title beside it); a tree's hero photograph is named "Open the photograph" for TalkBack and VoiceOver (it was an unnamed button).

## 2026-11-18 (session): "See all" on New from visitors in both apps

- **What changed**: Discover's "New from visitors" row now has "See all >" in the iOS and Android apps. It opens the website's /visitors page as an app screen: every photograph visitors sent that went live, newest sent first, as two columns of whole photographs (each at its own shape, not the row's 3:4 crop), the city tag on the photo and the visitor's name under it, 24 more loading as the end comes near, and "You have seen every photograph so far." at the end. A public name opens the profile; signed out the tap asks for a sign-in first and then finishes on that profile, as in the row.
- **How**: the server's `visitor_photos` keyset paging (already live). iOS Screens/VisitorPhotos.swift (the visitor line moved there so the row and the page share it), Kit/NewFromVisitors.page/author, Route.visitors, `-open=visitors:all`. Android ui/VisitorPhotos.kt (Compose staggered grid), the same paging in account/Visitors.kt, DiscoverPage.Visitors; the Android feed now reads each photograph's width and height (snapshot ATS4), so tiles take their shape before the picture loads; intro and end line in the seven languages from the website's strings. Added to ButtonWalk (both), SweepFrames, appsweep and android_parity's pairs (data/android-parity.json `visitors`).
- **Caught while building**: a page whose 24 photographs the feed did not carry yet left the spinner turning forever; a load cancelled by a sign-in counted as the end; and on a fresh install (iPhone SE, iOS 18, big text) the page stayed empty because the bundled feed did not know the newest photographs yet. All fixed on both platforms: the next page is asked at once, a stale load is dropped, and the tiles are read from the current feed as the page draws (as the row does), so they appear the moment the feed arrives.
- **Checked**: iPhone 17 Pro and iPhone SE (iOS 18.6, extra large text), signed out (a name asks for sign-in) and signed in with the test account (a name opens the profile; nothing written); Android emulator, fresh install, font 1.3; endless loading to the end line on iOS; the same first photographs in the same order on web, iPhone and Android. Not checked: airplane mode (no answer stops the page after its intro, as the website does).
- **For Hidde**: in the next TestFlight build and the Android build after 0.1.0 (2). Nothing archived, uploaded or submitted.

## 2026-11-18 (session): Android closed test submitted to Google

## 2026-11-18 session: app health read from PostHog, build number in every event, the repo left iCloud

- **App health (PostHog, App Store phones, 15 to 18 Nov)**: 2.3% of cold launches come within two minutes of the app being on screen (it died: crash, memory kill or a swipe), the same on iOS 18, 26 and 27; about half right after a tree page (median ~45 s), which fits the big-photo memory reading of 11-16. Query: `count where event='app_open' and gap<120s, group by properties.build_number`.
- **The repo moved to ~/Developer/Ancienttrees** (Hidde: "Verhuis de repo"). ~/Documents is iCloud-synced with Optimise Mac Storage on a 97%-full disk, so macOS evicted git's own files and `git worktree add` failed. Fresh clone from GitHub; local-only files copied across (out/ without out/tmp, private drafts, settings.local.json), git config (hooksPath, author, inflight merge driver), Claude memory, the daily publish task's path, CLAUDE.md. Android needs `android/local.properties` (sdk.dir=/Users/hidde/Library/Android/sdk) per checkout.
- **For Hidde**: open Xcode from ~/Developer/Ancienttrees/ios/AncientTrees/AncientTrees.xcodeproj from now on and start Claude sessions in ~/Developer/Ancienttrees. ## 2026-11-18 session: a sign-up to an address that can't receive mail is refused

- **Product, LIVE in the hook since about 10:30 UTC** (send-email v9): a sign-in or sign-up link to an address whose domain has no mail server and is a typo of a provider on data/email-domains.json (hoymail.com for hotmail.com), or whose domain does not exist at all, is refused instead of sent. Hidde, after a welcome mail to "hoymail.com" sat retrying for a day: the "Did you mean" warning "doesnt stop people from making the mistake". Benchmarked (CONVENTIONS.md "A mistyped email address", layer 3): the hard stop at sign-up is a mail-server lookup; nobody mails a guessed address after a bounce. A lookup that errs or is slow lets the mail through. qa's `check_one_email_domain_list()` now also holds the hook's copy of the list.
- **Website** (with this deploy): the sign-in sheet and /account say "That address can't receive mail." in all eight languages, and the field stays blocked until the address changes or the "Did you mean" address is tapped. **Apps**: blocked already through the server; they show their could-not-send line with the suggestion under it. For the next iOS and Android build: show `noMail` (site/src/lib/signin-strings.ts) when /auth/v1/otp answers 422 with `msg` starting "undeliverable:".
- Tested against the live hook: hoymail refused with the corrected address, a non-existent domain refused, our own address and a real domain sent; a real /auth/v1/otp call for a hoymail address comes back 422 "undeliverable:…@hotmail.com" and leaves no account behind. Not checked: the web message rendered in a browser (checked after the deploy).
- Slip: one test call sent a "Reset your password" mail with a dead link to test@mail.com; it bounced, so it reached nobody.
- The reader of 11-16 (hoymail, unconfirmed) signed up at 06:45 UTC, seven hours before the warning existed. Sent on Hidde's yes: one plain mail to the hotmail address through Resend (batch `signup-typo-2026-11-18`), "sign in again with this address", no sign-in link in it.
- Bounces since 11-01, read from the mailbox (bounce notices only): 15 addresses, two of them typo domains. The other, outlook.co for outlook.com (11-15), signed up again correctly a minute later and is active; her unconfirmed outlook.co account is left as it is. The rest were the Yahoo, Apple relay and BT route refusals already handled on 11-16 and 11-17, plus one magazine address.

## 2026-11-17 session: Android before the Play closed test (PR 64), found on Hidde's Samsung and by three walks

- **Product changes, all on claude/android-tabbar-proposal (PR 64), on Hidde's phone**: English only, as the iPhone (`localeFilters`, translations kept in res/values-xx; Hidde: "eens"); portrait only; camera photos also saved to Pictures/Ancient Trees (CameraRoll, as iOS keeps one in Photos); the keyboard no longer pushes the screen up (`adjustResize`); a Discover search no longer leaves the map without its menu; back from a page over the map leaves the page; back from a place link returns to the page it came from; a theme switch no longer restarts the app; Distances in miles reach every label; your own photos of our trees show as a row and as the hero, with delete in the viewer; tappable names on notes and "Photo: name"; refresh on return to the app; photo picker is Android's own again (Samsung Gallery via ACTION_PICK failed); My trees map draws your own trees in your photo; the gear and Find people share one size; the tab bar is one white ground with Polarsteps' soft top line.
- **A bug on both apps since launch**: every request said `Content-Type: application/json` even with no body, and Supabase storage refuses such a DELETE, so removing a photo, a tree's photo or an avatar (account deletion) left the file behind. Fixed in Android Supa.kt and iOS Account.swift (iOS from the next build). On Hidde's word ("Openbare kopieën weg") the 31 public copies (shared-sightings + thumbs) whose sightings row was gone and that nothing in data/ or site/ names were removed; 26 that the repo names and all 157 private files stay.
- **Ratchet**: FlowWalk `theKeyboardLeavesTheFieldInView` (also asserts portrait) and `aPlaceFromDiscoverSearchKeepsTheMenu`, both shown red with the fault put back; two escaped-bug rows closed with them. RELEASE_CHECKLIST.md "Every build" now has the three passes a session runs before Hidde tests a build (test-account walk incl. sending and cleanup; small old phone, big text, no network; screen pairs + app-level settings).
- **Test account**: a walk sent a real collect, a new tree and a note, then removed them; what the app could not remove (one sighting, submission 1110, four files) was removed by id, and the profile set back to private.
- **Rounds 2 and 3 (small phone at 130% text, no network; every screen beside the iPhone, 39 pairs)**: a full-height sheet now scrolls (Add note was under the keyboard); one map snapshot at a time and the map stopped before it is destroyed (two freezes on a GPU-less emulator, unconfirmed on a real phone); Take me there drops the distance rather than cutting it; Find people's reason on one line; number and unit never split; location asked once per install (it asked again every cold start after a refusal); the site's Open in app link (open.ancienttrees.app) opens the Android app; Kilometers spelled as on the iPhone.
- **For Hidde, not decided by a run**: signed-out My trees at 130% text on a small phone puts Sign in under the tab bar at the half stop (as the iPhone's half stop); the place-pin header is neither the iPhone's nor Android's form; the gallery copy of a camera photo carries the camera's own GPS only (iOS writes the phone's).
- **Open, not done**: offline town search says "check the spelling" on both apps; notes say "No notes yet" offline; Discover's visitor shelf does not reload when the network returns; debug builds read the catalogue in 17 to 50 s on a loaded emulator (release on the S21 is quick, not measured on a cheap phone); CI walk on PR 64 to be read again.

## 2026-11-17 session: BT refuses our sign-up mail through Gmail

Breakage: a reader who signed up at 18:45 UTC with a btinternet.com address never got in. Their welcome mail (with the confirm link) went through Gmail as info@ and BT answered "550 User unknown". Hidde's own Gmail mail to the same address at 20:18 UTC was taken, with no bounce. So the address is real and BT refuses mail that is unsigned for our domain, as Apple's relay did this afternoon. Four other BT accounts confirmed on 11-15, so BT does this only some of the time. Fix: btinternet.com, btopenworld.com and talk21.com join `SIGNED_ONLY_DOMAINS` in scripts/mailer.py and in the hook, and qa's `check_one_mail_route_rule()` holds btinternet.com. LIVE in the hook since 20:33 UTC: send-email v6 deployed with a fresh 7-day token Hidde made (it carries this afternoon's Apple relay line too, which had waited for a redeploy). Tested: a signed test welcome to our own Gmail alias arrived, an unsigned call is refused (401), the hook is still switched on. Not tested: a real BT inbox (we have none). Sent with Hidde's yes: a fresh sign-in link with an apology to that reader, through Resend only (batch `bt-signin-resend`).

Every bounce since 11-15 checked again (Hidde: "ga die ook nog maar eens na"), each against Supabase: ours were Yahoo's servers (fixed 11-16, resent), Apple's relay (fixed and resent today) and BT (above). Theirs, proven: outlook.co and a misspelt gmail.com address (both people signed up again correctly within minutes and are in), hoymail.com (no mail server at all, a typo of hotmail; that person never came back), a Google account whose own domain no longer exists (they sign in through Google, only our mails miss). Since the hook went live, Yahoo-family sign-ups confirm 4 of 6, others 71 of 80: no sign of a further fault, too few to be sure. Resend's delivery log cannot be read with our key (send-only).

For Hidde: 9 Yahoo-family sign-ups from 11-15/16 are still not in, after our two mails failed them. drafts/signin-sorry-yahoo-2026-11-23.md is one last apology with a working link, sendable from Monday 2026-11-23 (week's rest); it goes on your yes only. Hidde asked on 11-17 to be reminded in a day. Who: email sign-ups on Yahoo's servers (`YAHOO_FAMILY`) created 11-15 to 11-16 10:40 UTC and still unconfirmed on the day, picked fresh from Supabase (out/tmp/yahoo_after_fix.py shows them). How: a fresh magic link each (generate_link, redirect /account), Resend only, logged in data/outreach-sent.json, as out/tmp/bt_signin_link.py did for the BT reader. One mail each, never a second. Subject: "Your Ancient Trees sign-in link".

## 2026-11-17 session: Apple relay mails bounced, now through Resend

Breakage: since the 11-16 route split, mails to Sign in with Apple addresses (@privaterelay.appleid.com) went through Gmail, unsigned for our domain. Read from his Sent folder and bounces: about 30 went that way and 5 bounced "user not found", all from the reader judge's run at 17:19 UTC today (one of those addresses had taken a Gmail mail on 11-16), against none of 33 through Resend before. ## 2026-11-17 session: two more ways a signed-in person was asked to sign in

- **Web (Hidde: "kun je nagaan of er nog anders zon loop zit?")**: back on the site after more than an hour, the bar said signed in but save, collect, Add a tree (/contribute opened sign-in by itself) and /account asked to sign in. The access token lasts an hour; signin-js renews it on load, but the one door (collection-js verify) and those pages decided first and kept "signed out". Now signin-js exposes the renewal as `window.atRefreshing` and the door, /contribute, /account and /account/settings wait for it.
- **App**: signed in with no signal and a spent token, writing a note, following someone (two screens), asking to be an ambassador or joining Plus opened the sign-in sheet. No signal is not a sign-out (Account.apply), so these now say it did not go through; only a real sign-out asks. In the next build.
- qa `check_signed_in_is_never_asked_to_sign_in()` guards both (it flagged all five app places before the fix). Two escaped-bug rows, closed with it. Every other sign-in call on web and app was read and asks only when signed out.

## 2026-11-17 session: the reader system's holes closed

- **Deploy unblocked**: every deploy since 12:53 failed QA on a reader's raw account id in data/leads/_sightings.json (a photo path written by a reader judge run); now coded with people.ref. `check_no_reader_traces_in_the_repo()` moved into qa's source-only checks, so it refuses the push, not the deploy an hour later. The raw id stays in git history (no force-push, hard rule 3); it is an opaque account id, no name or address.

- **Alarm**: scripts/reader_backlog.py, read by health.py: anything a reader sent that waited over 24h without a verdict is RUNG 2. It found three at once (tips 802, 820, 850 from 11-15).

## 2026-11-17 session: one route for every reader's photograph

- **What changed**: a reader's photograph now always ends with a verdict as a tree of its own unless it shows one of ours (Hidde: "1 route"). Before, a photo sent for one of our trees could only be "this tree" or "rejected"; readers' trees died there (Dublin's weeping cedar arch in Glasnevin, an Azores fig curtained in aerial roots, a vast burred plane in London woods, El Sargento's dead ahuehuete in Chapultepec among them). Now: `other_tree` verdict in sightings_publish.py turns such a photo into a waiting lead (`to_lead()`, test in test_sightings.py); sightings_inbox.py sends every "not sure which tree" photograph down the lead route without the app's first-candidate name (`unsure_sightings()`, `unsure_among`); BRIEF_READER_JUDGE.md "One route".
- **Backfill, viewed here**: 13 photos set aside since September looked at again. 6 held under the closed list (Stockholm maple, Melbourne park tree, Paris square tree, Padua garden pagoda tree, Groningen beech: `not_special`; Bristol redwood: `bad_frame`). 7 worth a page go back through the intake for the reader judge to build: Dublin b59c4873, Azores abb593f0, Kanazawa pine 0942bd2b, the burred plane e7bef8af, Chiang Mai strangler fig c1046494, London plane 9c93b205, El Sargento fb383f1e (dead and standing: the 11-17 exception).
- Dublin's and Stockholm's queued "it is none of those" replies withdrawn.

## 2026-11-17 session: readers' trees judged within minutes (reader judge)

- **Built** (Hidde: "should be instant honestly can it just trigger a flow??", then "Yes, build it"): `.github/workflows/reader-judge.yml` starts on a `reader-tree` repository_dispatch (or by hand), waits ten minutes so a burst becomes one run, reads the inbox, fetches every waiting photograph, and an Opus agent judges and publishes per the new BRIEF_READER_JUDGE.md (closed list from CLAUDE.md), then a deploy is dispatched. At most one run waits behind a running one.
- **Fixed for every run**: `scripts/fetch_waiting_photos.py` puts the photograph of every waiting reader lead on the runner; the night run calls it too, so "files not on this runner" cannot recur. 27 older leads have their photos fetched on the first run.
- **No setup needed** (Hidde: "do i have to do all this or can you do parts of it?"): the workflow also polls every 15 minutes with `scripts/reader_poll.py` (two id lists, no photographs, so almost no Supabase egress); only a sighting not yet in sightings-processed.json or a tree submission from the last 25 minutes starts the rest. A tree is judged roughly 15 to 45 minutes after it is sent, GitHub's cron allowing. The Supabase trigger (supabase/reader-judge-setup.md) stays as an optional faster path, his to paste if he wants it; a session cannot create the token or run SQL on production.

Why: since the Guardian piece app phones opened there (GeoIP: 30, 28, 25) with 1 or 2 of our trees within 25 km. ## 2026-11-16 session: an app crash in the sheet, readers' notes acted on, Nick asked to be Bristol's ambassador

- **App crash fixed (next build)**: ios.yml went red twice today with a different unit test "crashed with signal abrt" each time. The crash report in the result bundle (AncientTrees-2026-11-16-152543.ips) shows the app itself aborting: "pointer being freed was not allocated" in Swift's back-deployed deinit-on-executor hop, under `SheetDragClock`'s deinit (BottomSheet.swift). Under default MainActor isolation the class's deinit hopped to the main actor, and @State throws a new clock away on nearly every sheet redraw. Now `nonisolated` (08f53480a). Could also hit people in the store build; whether it explains the 1.5% quick reopens shows once PostHog's crash capture ships in the same build. No unit test reproduces it on demand (a runtime race on dealloc); ios.yml is the check.

## 2026-11-16 session: reports and moved pins get their own table (step 2 of splitting submissions)

- **What changes** (Hidde: the catch-all `submissions` is "een troep", step 2 "later als het rustiger is"): supabase/tree-reports.sql makes `tree_reports` (the name `reports` is taken by reports about a person). One row per report: tree, reason (the chip, or pin moved, collected far, merged, typed correction), the person's follow-up words filled IN PLACE instead of a second "report detail" row, the coordinate of a moved pin, and the verdict and reply fields (outcome, reply_text, replied_at, thanked_at). Own-rows RLS (a reader may only insert a bare report, never a verdict), cascade on account deletion, GRANT in the file, ids from 1,000,001 so they never equal a submissions id. A trigger on `submissions` moves every report, follow-up and correction posted there (every build out today, and the website, posts there) across and drops it from submissions with a 201 to the sender. The 68 existing rows are copied with their verdicts and replies (only accounts that still exist; 3 rows without an account stay readable in submissions); nothing deleted. Trees sent, ambassador requests and general feedback stay in submissions.
- **Reading**: website (worthit-js.ts), iPhone (`MyVotes.standingReports`) and Android (`MyVotes.standingReports`) read your reports from `tree_reports`, the old reading as fallback until the paste; the apps carry it in their next build. Senders are unchanged (the trigger routes them). Scripts read both tables through scripts/tree_reports.py (submissions row shape, each report once, PATCH back to the table it came from): contributor_reply.py, inbox.py, show_digest.py readers in full, daily_digest.py (every feedback count), reader_words.py, and claude_gate.py, so a report still wakes the knock. qa `check_reports_are_read_from_their_own_table()`. privacy-map maps the file to "Suggestions, corrections and votes".
- **Live in Supabase** (Hidde pasted it the same evening). Checked: of 68 report rows in submissions, 56 copied and the 8 follow-ups folded into their report (so 56 rows in tree_reports, 43 with a pin coordinate), verdicts and replies carried with no row losing one; 3 rows without an account stay in submissions, 1 of a deleted account was not copied. Round trip on the test account: a report posted to submissions gets 201 and lands in tree_reports with no submissions row, its follow-up fills the same row, a pin move lands with its coordinate; the account reads its own reports back, sees nobody else's, and is refused (403) when it tries to write a verdict. Test rows deleted.

Hidde: in the iOS app the first four tiles of "New from visitors" had no photograph, on the web every tile had one. Cause: the server names a reader's photograph the moment it is published (bri_018, bos_047, bil_004, lef_001, all today 11:39 to 13:12), the feed only after the next deploy; those four trees had no photograph in the live feed yet. The website skips a tree its build does not know; the apps drew the tree without a picture. Now both apps use the website's rule: a tile only when the feed already carries that visitor's photograph (`NewFromVisitors.tiles` in Kit/NewFromVisitors.swift and account/Visitors.kt). Reaches phones with the next iOS and Android builds; the four tiles appear with their photographs after the deploy now running. iOS compiled locally; Android left to CI (no SDK on the Mac).

## 2026-11-16 session: how often the app really ends on screen

- **Why**: MetricKit reported one store crash from ~4,000 new phones over the Guardian weekend. It only reports from phones that share analytics and never reports memory kills; meanwhile 58 of 4,183 phones reopened the app within two minutes, 53 of those reopens right after a tree page (spread over many trees, all iOS versions, median 48 s after opening).
- **Product (next iOS build, and Android before its release)**: every run that ends on screen now sends one `app_exit_abnormal` event to PostHog with the last event and tree (Kit/ExitWatch.swift, the Crashlytics-style unclean-shutdown flag; Android reads ApplicationExitInfo). Apple's daily foreground exit counts (memory, bad access, watchdog) go in as `app_exit_counts` instead of being thrown away. Photos are decoded at most 2048 px (ImageIO thumbnail decode): 1,172 feed photos are Wikimedia originals of 20 to 50 MP, about 100 MB each in memory the old way; no visible change. PhotoMemoryTests green; Android compiles.
- **Not proven**: big originals are not over-represented before the reopens (69% vs 64% of all tree opens), so they are a risk removed, not the confirmed cause. The new events will say after the build is out.

## 2026-11-16 session: one mail route rule (Yahoo and first mails signed, the rest through Gmail)

- Every mail now picks its route by recipient and kind (scripts/mailer.py, `wants_resend()`): Resend for addresses on Yahoo's servers and for first mails to strangers (outreach_send.py, `mailer.FIRST`); Gmail for mail people asked for or expect (contributor_reply, sightings_publish, ambassador: `mailer.EXPECTED`); either route takes over when the other refuses or cannot be reached. Builds on the Gmail fallback below. Tested against fake servers (scripts/test_mailer.py, nine cases); no real mail sent by this change.
- The Supabase hook (supabase/functions/send-email/index.ts) follows the same rule and the same Yahoo list; qa `check_one_mail_route_rule()` keeps the lists equal and reader scripts on EXPECTED. Rule in CLAUDE.md (Reader input), why in DECISIONS.md.
- LIVE since 10:40 UTC: the Send Email hook is deployed (send-email v2, JWT off, signature checked) and switched on through the Management API, with a 7-day token Hidde made. Supabase's SMTP setting (Gmail) stays as the fallback when the hook is switched off. The hook now carries the dashboard's own templates verbatim (`python3 scripts/mail_hook_templates.py` copies them in; run it and redeploy after editing a template in the dashboard). Tested: an unsigned call refused (401); a real sign-up and a real sign-in link to Gmail aliases arrived via the hook, both links signed in and confirmed (303 to /account with tokens); Resend's API accepted the hook's key. Both test accounts deleted. Not tested: a real Yahoo inbox (we have none); the first Yahoo sign-up shows in Resend's Emails log.
- Follow-up sent with Hidde's yes (about 11:50 CET): Yahoo's servers refused mail to 16 addresses since 11-15 (yahoo.*, aol.com, sky.com); 14 still had no confirmed account (4 of them had the 11-15 link mail, expired). Each got a fresh sign-in link through Resend, "the problem is fixed". sky.com added to the Yahoo list in mailer.py and the hook (redeployed). No bounce since the hook went live.
- Hidde: links valid for 24 hours ("make it valid for 24 hours for them"): Supabase's `mailer_otp_exp` 3600 -> 86400. It is one project-wide setting, so every sign-in and welcome link now lasts a day; Supabase checks the age at click time, so the 14 links sent that morning count too.
- Undo: `python3 out/tmp/hook_switch.py off` (scratch) or Authentication > Hooks > Send Email off in the dashboard.
- Seen while there: Supabase's email rate limit is 30 an hour (`rate_limit_email_sent`); the Guardian morning's busiest hour had 25 email sign-ups plus sign-in links, so some people may have hit "rate limit exceeded". Raising it is Hidde's call.

## 2026-11-16 session: mail goes through Gmail when Resend is full

Resend warned at 80% of its free 100 a day. Reason: the Guardian wave. On 11-15, about 84 script mails went through Resend (59 photo-live, 15 replies, 6 Yahoo sign-ins, 4 ambassador). The 129 welcome mails went through Gmail, so they don't count. Past 100, Resend refused and the scripts retried a day later. Now (Hidde: "Yes, add fallback") scripts/mailer.py sends a mail Resend refuses, and the rest of that connection, through Gmail, as the Supabase sign-in hook already does. A dropped connection does not fall back (the mail may have left). Gmail mail is unsigned for our domain, so a Yahoo address still bounces on a full day. Tested against fake servers (refusal, unreachable, no Gmail route raises). Watch the month: the free plan stops at 3,000.

## 2026-11-16 session: the red "Maximum call stack" web error is not ours

- The quality line's red Web Live cell ("RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded.", 5 reports on 3 pages, 11-15) comes from one client, not our code. All 5 rows say Chrome 157, from two bursts (09:43 and 19:59 UTC) by the same client that also sent 7 "Failed to fetch" rows and an "Error: Na" that day. Lines 190 to 194 point at unrelated code on each page (a comment on /, an `if (!dlg) return` on /explore, a headers() builder on /bilbao); none of it can recurse. It is a script injected by an extension or automated browser, reported with our page as its file. /, /explore and /bilbao run without a console error in Chrome today.
- No filter added: "Maximum call stack" in FOREIGN would hide a real loop of ours. The cell counts 24 hours and goes off by itself around 20:00 UTC on 11-16. If it comes back from more than one browser version, it is real.
- `window.atDownsize is not a function` on /account/settings (3 reports, 3 browsers, 11-15) was already fixed in d0a57c1fd, live since 20:39 UTC; every report predates it.

## 2026-11-15 session: the Guardian day's first minute

- **Product**: the app now measures a pin tap (`pin_tapped`, tree id or `own`) and the first finger move on the map per launch (`map_moved`), iOS and Android, in the next build of each (commit 5f8a7dfcd). Nothing visible changes.
- Why: of 2,314 new phones by 14:45, 41% never fired `tree_opened`; 722 of those sent only `app_open` and `location` (allowed in 6 s). No technical pattern (same iOS versions, same allow rate, 37 to 44% in every hour); a tree within 5 km still left 36% without one, beyond 50 km 51%. Those who did open a tree took a median 44 s to reach it. `tree_opened` fires only on the full page, while a pin already shows the card, so 41% is a ceiling. The first screen is Hidde's design call; nothing changed there.
- Not done: the iOS CI was already red on main before this; this change does not touch it.

## 2026-11-15 session: Yahoo blocks the welcome mail

- Broken: Supabase sends its auth mails through Hidde's Gmail as info@ancienttrees.app (SMTP set 2026-09, drafts/auth-mails.md). Gmail signs for gmail.com, so Yahoo refuses them ("550 5.7.9 ... requires this sender to authenticate with DKIM"). On the Guardian day 6 email sign-ups on Yahoo addresses (8 bounces) never got their welcome mail and stayed unconfirmed; Gmail, Outlook and the rest arrive.
- Sent with Hidde's yes: one mail to each of the 6 through Resend (signed for our domain), with a fresh sign-in link from Supabase's generate_link, saying from tomorrow Yahoo mail works again. Logged as batch yahoo-signin-2026-11-15.
- Not chosen: moving Supabase's SMTP to Resend. Resend is on the free plan (100 a day) and the day had 71 email sign-ups in four hours, so on a press day every email sign-up would fail past the hundredth. Resend Pro ($20 a month) is too dear for Hidde.
- Built, NOT live: supabase/functions/send-email/index.ts, a Send Email hook that routes Yahoo-family addresses (Yahoo, AOL, Verizon and the rest on Yahoo's servers) through Resend and everyone else through Gmail SMTP on 465 (Edge Functions block 25 and 587), each falling back to the other. For Hidde tonight: deploy in the dashboard, four secrets, Auth > Hooks > Send Email on, test with a Gmail and a Yahoo address. Copy the dashboard's current templates in first if they differ from drafts/auth-mails.md. The mail promised the 6 "from tomorrow".

## 2026-11-13 session: Supabase egress back under the free plan

Supabase mailed that the org passed the free plan's 5 GB egress this month (grace until 2026-12-13, then possible restrictions on accounts, saves and uploads). Hidde: no paying for now, and no smaller photographs ("fix maar").
- **Backup**: db-backup.yml takes every stored file once in 28 days and only changed files in the weeks between (`-new` artifacts; BACKUP_RESTORE.md says how to restore). Tested: a week costs 24 MB instead of 145.
- **Tiles**: `scripts/shared_thumbs.py` (night run, after sightings_link) keeps a 600 px copy of each shared photo in the new public bucket `shared-thumbs`; 127 made, 83 KB average against 934. Profile tiles on web, iOS and Android load it and fall back to the full copy; the shared tree page keeps the full photograph. iOS and Android take effect with their next builds.
- **Avatars**: two oversized ones re-encoded to 512 px (476 and 117 KB to 43 and 69); new uploads were already 512.
- **The real source, found after Hidde pasted the usage page** (6.0 GB used 10-28 to 11-13, about 375 MB a day, with 19 active users, so machines, not people): `private_store.py` read the whole private_files table (6.4 MB) four times a knock, about 160 MB a day. Now gzip (5.8 MB to 1.2 on the wire), and a push reads back only files changed since the last pull (snapshot in out/), so a quiet knock's pushes read nothing. `crashes.py` (health and quality: 6 MB of MetricKit payloads) asks for gzip too.
- Restriction comes from 2026-12-13 if the cycle 11-28 to 12-28 also passes 5 GB, so about 165 MB a day is the ceiling. Check the usage page around 2026-11-20 for the new daily level.

The bookmark is moss instead of red (it kept the heart's red; the cards' bookmark is moss), the collected tick is a filled check-circle (Phosphor on the web, Apple's checkmark.circle.fill in the app) instead of a line tick, the ticket stays blue but is now the same 15 disc in the app (was 17) and Phosphor's ticket fill on the web (was a hand-made rounded rectangle), the counterpart of the app's ticket.fill. Web and iPhone in the same change; the iPhone shows it from the next build.

## 2026-11-12 session: a sign-in ends where the tap was going

- **Fixed (Hidde: tapping Isabelle R. on the home page asked for a sign-in and then landed back on the home page):** the website's sign-in sheet now keeps the exact address it was opened on and, for a visitor's name, whose photograph was tapped; after Google, Apple or the mail link the page goes there and finishes the act, so the name opens the profile. The same cause broke Follow on somebody's profile: Supabase returns to the path without its query, so `/profile?u=X` came back as your own `/profile` and the follow was dropped. Both fixed in signin-js.ts (head relay) and visitors-row-js.ts. Checked in a fake browser (jsc): home, profile with query, an unrelated page, an off-site address refused.
- **Apps:** iPhone (Home.swift `openVisitor`) and Android (Discover.kt `openVisitor`) asked for a sign-in on the same name and then did nothing; the sign-in now re-asks the row with the new session and opens the profile, and the row reloads on any sign-in. Every other gate in both apps already finishes its act (save, collect, map filters, add photo).
- qa `check_a_sign_in_ends_where_the_tap_was_going()`; escaped bug ESC-2026-11-12-a-sign-in-asked-by-a-tap.
- **Tested with the test account (apptest@), live:** website home, tap a visitor's name signed out, sign in: lands on her profile (Isabel R.). Website profile, Follow signed out, sign in: back on her profile, Following, row on the server, removed after. iPhone simulator: name, sign in, her profile opens. Android: CI green, not walked on a device. **Breakage on the way:** the first version failed two deploys (qa: the head read the stored session before catching the token, fixed 669cf1c2f), and the home name first stopped one step short (the profile loader was not ready yet; it now retries when the page has loaded, 73c5a6c98).

## 2026-11-12 session: Android opens faster, and its sheets grow like the iPhone's

What changed in the Android app:
- The map shows its first trees sooner. On the emulator with a release build, the first pins came after 9 to 13 s on a first launch and 4.5 to 7.5 s after that; now 3.1 s and 2.3 s (median of five each, measured with nothing else on the emulator; the older numbers were taken while other sessions shared it). The iPhone simulator on the same Mac takes 1.5 to 1.7 s and 1.1 to 1.3 s. Two causes fixed: the catalogue only started loading once the screen was drawn (it now starts with the app), and the map's style was fetched from the network every launch (it now comes from the copy on the phone, kept fresh in the background, with one built into the app for the very first launch).
- That leaves Android about 1.5 s behind on a first launch and 1.1 s on a relaunch, mostly MapLibre starting its map; a real phone has not been measured.
- Sign-in and the other fitted sheets now drag up to the full height and back, rubber-banding at the top, as on the iPhone (audit row 12). A drag down from the fitted height still closes them.

Breakage: none known. The emulator's swipe injector sometimes delivers the lift late, so a fast flick reads as a stop there; the logic was checked with flicks that arrived on time and with slow drags.

## 2026-11-11 session: Android's icons and type as the iPhone's

What changed in the Android app (the review's icon and type rows, out/parity-feel/visual-review-2026-11-11.md):
- Sheets: iOS 26 draws a fitted sheet at full width and scaled about 4 percent smaller; Android now does the same (Sheet.kt). That was the "Gabarito 4 percent too wide": outside sheets the titles already matched to 1 dp. Text breaks as Apple's does: a two-line text never ends on one word ("Are you enjoying / Ancient Trees?", "...and the / Privacy notice."), a longer one may.
- Species silhouettes are flat and one-tone, as the iPhone draws them; the wingnut no longer reads as a weeping crown. The ticket badge on paid-entry pins draws again (its glyph was off the disc).
- Glyphs: the My trees leaf, curved directions arrow, outline sparkles for Plus, the early adopter's sparkle, an empty speech bubble for Send feedback, the needle pin in the pin picker, building icon in search, the round flat flags (the iPhone's flag-icons set), and sizes and weights read from the Swift (chips, card bookmark, close cross, tile glyphs, sheet buttons, tree page camera and thumbs, season tiles, Settings rows and chevrons, gear and Find people).
- Type: search sets only the typed letters heavy, cultivar names upright, card meta lines 13 with the faint dot and the species giving way first, no more mid-word clipping of two-line card titles, navigation titles in Gabarito, Form sheets on the system's cool grey with 26 corners and the system's placeholder grey.

Not changed, on purpose: the My trees gear still fills as the sheet rises. The Swift does that (Hidde, 2026-10-08); the iPhone frames show it outline only because the progress never reaches the button there, an iPhone bug.

## 2026-11-10 Android measures what people do, like the iPhone

- Android now sends the iPhone's PostHog events to the same project with the same properties (account/Measure.kt, a port of Kit/Measure.swift): app_open, tab, tree_opened (tree, near), tree_saved, tree_visited, sync_failed, sighting_recorded, directions, share_tapped (map_card, tree, collect, your_photo, profile), location, plus-open, plus-answer, plus-tellme. `os` reads "Android 15" beside "iOS 26", so scripts/posthog.py and the digest count both phones with no change; a debug install says `build: debug` and the digest leaves it out as it does for Xcode installs.
- No PostHog SDK, on purpose: the iPhone deliberately uses none (Measure.swift: the package brings its own networking, session replay and flags we do not want), so Android sends the same one HTTP POST, the way Supa.kt talks to the server. Nothing added to the build.
- Emulators stay silent unless `-measure` is passed, the iPhone's simulator rule, so parity shots and CI walks are never counted.
- On the phone: the install id and the offline queue under the iPhone's keys (measure.install.v1, measure.queue.v1), both already ruled `device` in data/cross-device.json.
- Privacy page: "the app and iOS version" now reads "the version of the app and of your phone's system (iOS or Android)". privacycheck.py green.
- The tree page's last parity difference (tree_opened not sent) is closed in data/android-parity.json.
- Not on Android because the iPhone feature is not there yet: sync_skipped (Android cannot heart signed out; it asks to sign in first).

## 2026-11-10 Android app links: assetlinks.json

- The site now publishes `/.well-known/assetlinks.json` for `app.ancienttrees.android`, so Android can verify the manifest's `autoVerify` filter and open ancienttrees.app links in the app, as the iPhone does with apple-app-site-association.
- It carries one fingerprint today: the debug key on Hidde's Mac (~/.android/debug.keystore, SHA-256 D8:7C:...:2C:09), which signs every build installed on the emulator from this Mac. There is no upload or release key in the repo yet; CI's debug APKs are signed with a throwaway key and stay browser-only.
- **For Hidde, once:** when the first build goes to Play, Play Console > Setup > App signing shows the "App signing key certificate" SHA-256 (and the upload key's). Both get added to `sha256_cert_fingerprints` in site/public/.well-known/assetlinks.json; until then a Play-installed app opens shared links in the browser.

## 2026-11-10 write pass: two Vienna Prater trees go into the app, everything else on the shelf is still held

- **Product:** vie_060, the Prater Meadow Oak on the Kaiserallee (Naturdenkmal 867, 4.66 m round, more than twice any oak within 45 m, confirmed pin), and vie_061, the Heustadelwasser Lime (ND 868, 2.95 m, confirmed pin, best_time July flowers), are written and merged into data/cities/vienna.json. Under APP FIRST both are app-only. Their stories hedge the inventory planting years (1841, 1913) as estimates. The lime's story says plainly that we have not confirmed a public path to the trunk, and points readers to the water's side, where the register says it is seen from afar.
- **Refused, all held by earlier passes for closed-list reasons that nothing new has answered:** asp_001 and asp_002 (beech trunks known only by locality, so they cannot be told apart), ssb_002 and ssb_003 (same, silver firs), brd_001 (hospital courtyard behind a gate, hard rule 10), frk_027 (schoolyard, no viewing point sourced), inn_007 (pin on a private address), cbu_001 (a doline at 1,612 m with no route), dmr_002 (no sign it has stood since a 2019 decline report), sgz_005 and not_002 (no path or land tenure), not_003 (church grounds, access unknown), tmt_001 (a register group with access unknown), kcm_005 (no recognition line, photo or confirmed pin, so the build refuses it), sfo_012 (the same tree as live sfo_004). Taiwan's "pending" row is a `_notes` object, not a tree.
- **READY leads not written:** all seven are unverified finding aids. The St. Louis elm and willow are single-source and one is ticketed, the Tucson fig and jacaranda carry an unconfirmed champion claim and no story, the Windsor oaks are three register rows from 2008, the Segovia sequoia is one of six similar trunks behind a fence, and the Vienna Grimmelshausengasse oak has no age or girth. Each needs a verify pass first.
- Preflight: 0 problems; pace check clean.

The sheet has the iPhone's four stops and its drag rules (below full the sheet moves, at full the list scrolls, down from the top of the list brings it back), a tap on the peek raises it, and a flick lands on the nearest stop. Search field fixed at the top, the four chips (Want to visit and Collected ask for sign-in first), the count under the grip, Highlights shelf, a Collected shelf, every tree in view as a row, the tapped pin's card with share and close, the Map pill at full height, the locate arrow and the location-off chip riding the sheet. ## 2026-11-10 session: compliance audit, privacy page and terms brought up to date, weekly compliance light

- Privacy page rewritten to what the product does: every account has a profile, public unless switched off, and what a public profile shows (Collected and Want to go included); notes on trees and the "new from visitors" row are public with a public profile's name; shared links; suggested people. Added what GDPR article 13 requires and the page lacked: who decides, why we may use each kind of data, how long we keep it, who handles it (Supabase, PostHog, Google, Apple, Resend, ImprovMX, GitHub, OpenFreeMap), rights and the Autoriteit Persoonsgegevens, 16 and over.
- Terms: 16 and over, profiles public by default, notes public, a name beside a photo in the visitors row; the false "nothing beside it says who took it, ever" and "your collection is shown to nobody" are gone.
- Photo credits on the website: the photographer's name links to the source page (Commons, iNaturalist, Geograph, or attribution_url) and the licence label to its deed (CreditLine.astro). CC 2.0 and 3.0 give no grace period for a missing link, which is what claim agencies invoice.
- First outreach mails must end with a way to say no: mailcheck refuses one without it, and OUTREACH_STRATEGY.md says so. The batches of 11-01 to 11-06 had none.
- So this stops depending on Hidde asking: scripts/privacycheck.py on every push (a new table needs its privacy sentence), a weekly compliance review in review.yml (BRIEF_COMPLIANCE.md), and a compliance part in the Next version light of Web and iOS (quality.py). The light is orange now, on purpose: six points wait for Hidde in data/compliance.json.
- For Hidde: compliance, 6 points wait for your word (data/compliance.json): the 33 accounts made public on 10-29, public by default at sign-up, your full name or KvK on the privacy page, PostHog without consent, mailed photos live before the yes, mail sentences quoted without asking.

## 2026-11-06 evening session: the explore map crashed the tab

**Breakage, fixed.** Since 476ee80 (this afternoon's "the map names the place in view") /explore crashed the browser tab a few seconds after loading (Chrome "Error code 5"), on desktop and phone, anywhere on the map; Hidde hit it looking at the United Kingdom. Cause: the new trees list painted its save hearts with `atPaintSaves`, which on explore refilters the map, which redraws the list, forever. The list now paints the hearts alone (`atPaintSaveButtons`, tree-actions-js.ts). Found by pausing the live page in a debugger; the live page with the same patch stayed up at 1280 and 375 px with "United Kingdom" and its rows. The /united-kingdom page itself never crashed. db1416a2f.

**How it slipped through, and what now stops it** (Hidde: "doe het"). qa.py and preflight read files and never run the JavaScript; the smoke test runs Chrome but after the site is live, and its hang on /explore was read as a fault of the test (d3983b8). Three parts, all live with this push: (1) `scripts/pagecheck.py` opens nine pages (explore over the UK among them) in Chrome at desktop and phone width and fails on a crash, a hang or an error from our own scripts; deploy.yml runs it before the upload, so a broken build never replaces a working site. Run against the live site before the fix, it failed exactly the four explore lines and passed the rest. (2) rollback.yml puts back the previous build in about five minutes without building (uploads now kept seven days). (3) pages-live.yml runs the same check on the live site every hour into the quality line and health.py. Ratchet: `check_the_pages_run_before_they_go_live()`. The deploy gets a few minutes longer.

Hidde, on the four-week freeze below: "dit is mss wel de moeite omdat we dan hopelijk via de app meer bomen gefotografeerd worden". So the freeze (`SUPPLY_PAUSED`) is off again and the pace of 25 a day goes on, for the app; the website freezes by structure, and a new place of app-only trees has no place page.
- **How:** `treeIsRenderable` (site/src/lib/trees.ts, the gate every page, pin, card, sitemap and index goes through) now excludes app-only trees; the app's feeds use the new `treeIsInFeed`/`feedTreesOf`/`cityIsInFeed`/`walkableFeedTrees`; pins.json, cards.json and map-species.json are the website's own and stay on the web gate. The rule: not in data/web-freeze-baseline.json (4,046 ids live today), no reader signal (reader_added), no visible note; `treeIsAppOnly` in site/src/lib/app-first.ts and `app_only()` in scripts/findable.py (thin_pages.renderable reads it). An app-only tree's feed `url` is `/<city>#tree=<id>` (the city map opens that card), so a share from the app lands somewhere true. qa `check_an_app_only_tree_stays_off_the_web()`: no page, no sitemap line, id in /api/trees.json.
- **Verified:** thin_pages and crosscheck unchanged locally (no app-only tree exists yet); the build on main is the test, then one shelf tree goes live as the first app-only tree and is checked on the deployed site. Not yet covered: species.json counts and the collections in browse.json still count web trees only (minor, noted); an app user's saved app-only tree on the web profile has no page to link, which v2 can point at the city map.

## 2026-11-05 session: the four-week freeze on new pages of our own (Hidde: "ok voer dit door"; superseded by APP FIRST above the same afternoon)

- **Diagnosis, measured:** the 11-04 fall hit open pages (300 to 74 impressions) and tagged pages (1,049 to 238) alike, minus 75% on both in one hour, so Google re-applied a site-level judgement; our tags played no part (new section 0 of seo-diagnose.yml). Crawl stats (Hidde's Search Console): ~145 requests a day over 90 days, 79% HTML, 38% discovery, so a third of Google's reading went to pages we had just made while /lisbon was last read 10-08 and /united-states 08-09. On that pace the noindex clean-up reaches Google early next year, after the update expected within weeks.
- **What changed in the product:** nothing a reader sees. `SUPPLY_PAUSED = True` (findable.py): no new tree or place of our own until 2026-12-03, a reader's tree still ships; preflight and the pre-push hook refuse a new own tree against data/web-freeze-baseline.json (4,046 ids live today). The night run keeps researching and writing into data/research/, the shelf fills, and ships it at 25 a day once lifted. The pace cap stays as the guard for then.
- **Next, Hidde's plan:** redirect the 439 question pages to their city pages and the translations Google showed nothing of since 11-01 to their English twins (~1,560 of 1,802), and drop the links to them; keep the list of ~940 pages Google itself showed (data/tagged-still-shown.json) for a two-day release when it turns; look again 2026-11-18. App-only trees (feed yes, page no) are possible but not a switch: web and app read the same browse/trees/walks feeds, so about ten web consumers would need a filter; put to Hidde.

- **What changed:** 29 open-licence photographs, each looked at on a contact sheet, now sit where the builders read them, so these trees go live with a picture when the daily publish reaches them. US 14: New York 5 (Morningside sycamore, Van Cortlandt white ash, Weeping Beech Park cedar, Isham Park ginkgo, property-line white oak; in data/research/us-east-photos.json, new), Santa Barbara Moreton Bay fig, Kauai Allerton figs, Tyler Arboretum sequoia, Miami Bayside banyan, Salem Waldo Park tree, Tacoma Mountaineer Tree, Madison Oakland Ave hackberry, Tucson Engineering cork oak, Boulder Naropa sycamores. UK 15: London 4 (Harlington Yew, Maids of Honour pine, Grimston's Oak, George Green chestnuts), Glasgow Covenanters Oak, Mottisfont plane, Welsh and Cheshire yews 4 (Bettws Newydd, Nevern bleeding yew, Gresford, Speke Hall Adam and Eve), Castlewellan redwoods, Crom yews, Somerset and Dorset yews 3 (Compton Dundon, Lytchett Matravers, Wimborne St Giles). Held trees with a photo: US 13 -> 27 of 309 (4% -> 9%), UK 41 -> 56 of 138 (30% -> 41%).
- **How:** `photo_hunt.candidates_for` (Commons name and geosearch, iNaturalist, Openverse) on 395 photo-less held trees, Geograph's keyless syndicator (`<species> near lat,lon`) for the UK, a tight iNat pass (same genus within 25 m), then the session's own eyes on 48 contact sheets. Sources: Commons (many Geograph imports), Geograph direct (2), iNaturalist CC0/CC BY (9), Flickr CC BY / CC0 via Openverse (2). Reasons for each sit in the tree's verify_notes ("Photo pass 2026-11-04") or in the side photo files. scripts/build_us_hawaii.py and build_us_northwest.py now pass an approved photo through (they forced "missing").
- **Held on purpose:** gla_011 John Knox's Yew (the only photo is the 1981 slide the writing pass held), sua_001 New Place mulberry (the frame may show Peggy Ashcroft's 1969 tree), aus_011 Central Market Oak (iNat 2 m from the pin, but the trunk itself is unproven; approve and confirm the pin if the stone in the frame is the 2008 award sign). No pins upgraded.
- **Tested in place:** all fourteen touched builders, city_names, preflight: the photos land (29 of 29 approved in the built files); preflight fails on pace plus five failures from other sessions' new builders (prefix `wol` shared by Wolverhampton and Woolland, Scottsdale title and meta length, five two-name species in the UK West and Southwest batches). Reverted, city-aliases restored.
- **Not committed by me:** tucson-verified.json, boulder-verified.json and uk-ni-highlands-photos.json are other sessions' untracked files; their photos (tus_002, bld_003, cwl_001, crm_001) are written in them and go in with those sessions' commits.
- **Empty angles:** most held New York, Boston, DC and Leesburg trees have no geotagged or named open photo at all; Boston iNat hits are leaf and cone close-ups. Cambridge Botanic Garden, Bath Sydney Gardens and Westonbirt Geograph hits are generic views.
- **Cost:** about 320k tokens, roughly 11k per approved photo.

## 2026-11-04 session: watch the Google recovery, decide around 11-18

- **Agreed with Hidde ("we moeten het gewoon in de gaten houden"):** no further change to what Google sees. Around 2026-11-18 compare two weeks of clicks on open pages against clicks on tagged pages (data/tagged-still-shown.json). Open pages holding or rising while tagged ones drop: the cuts were right, keep the slow lane. The total sliding back towards ~35 impressions a day: the tagged pages were carrying it, put faster release to Hidde, demand-cut cities first. Verified live today: the three earned pages lost the googlebot tag and are in sitemap.xml (264 urls).

## 2026-11-04 session: three pages earned their way back into Google

- **What changed:** /tenerife/el-drago-milenario, /olympic-national-park/duncan-cedar and /malaga leave the googlebot noindex from the next deploy. Google kept showing them after 11-01 with clicks (43/1, 20/1, 16/1). The rule (`EARNED_BACK`, Hidde: "Ja, zet aan"): 2 clicks, or 1 click and 10 impressions since 11-01; no translations, question, listing or thin-place pages; at most 10 a week. Undo: `EARNED_BACK = False`, or take a path out of data/earned-back.json.

## 2026-11-04 session: most of Google's recovery is on pages we tagged noindex

- **Measured** (new `scripts/tagged_shown.py`, daily from data-digest.yml into data/tagged-still-shown.json, also printed by seo-diagnose.yml): from 11-01 to 11-03, 867 pages carrying the googlebot noindex earned 2,305 impressions and 31 clicks, against about 2,400 and 45 for the whole site. The tag is live (checked on /tenerife/el-drago-milenario); Google has simply not recrawled them, some for weeks, and now ranks them again. As it recrawls, this recovery will shrink by our own tag: read a fall in the digest against this file before calling it a new demotion.
- **Decided with Hidde:** change nothing for now, measure; releasing pages that proved themselves is the release lane's job later. His question on the plan's downside is answered in the session; no switch flipped.

## 2026-11-04 session: Google's jump and Bing's 11-01 spike, read

- **Google:** clicks back to about 23 a day (11-02), against 45 to 60 before 09-28; average position from ~25 to ~9 from 11-01, across the whole site at once. No cut was lifted: the Google sitemap holds 261 urls (about 5,000 before the demotion). But 973 pages earned an impression in ten days, among them /de and /fr/lyon/..., which carry the googlebot noindex: Google keeps showing a tagged page until it recrawls it, so clicks per open page cannot be read from these numbers yet.
- **Bing 11-01 (2,225 impressions, 8 clicks):** new read-only `scripts/bing_diagnose.py` and `.github/workflows/bing-diagnose.yml` (run by hand). Daily traffic is the only feed that has 11-01; Bing's query and page stats come in weekly buckets, the newest dated 10-30, so no query or page explains the spike yet. Nothing else moved: clicks normal, index growing steadily (3,740 to 3,868), biggest single query that day 18 impressions. Re-run the workflow once the bucket after 10-30 exists (from about 11-07) before reading anything into it. The digest's Bing query line sorts by clicks, so it can never show an impression spike.

## 2026-11-04 session: Kauai life check after Hurricane Lowell (rung 3), all nine unknown, nothing retired

**What changed in the product (once built):** the six live Kauai trees (Yamamoto and Gulick-Rowell monkeypods, Koloa baobab, Kanoa and Wilcox banyans, Tree Tunnel) say in `access` that Hurricane Lowell brought down many trees on Kauai in September 2026, that we have no word on this one since, and to check before a special trip. `verify_notes` carry what was searched.

**Finding:** no report names any of our Kauai trees as fallen, and none has a dated photograph or observation after 2026-09-08 either. Searched county and HDOT updates, Civil Beat, Kauai Now, Hawaii News Now, NTBG, iNaturalist near each pin (people did observe other species within 200 m of several in October). Maluhia Road was listed closed on 09-08 with no reason given; the tunnel is hundreds of trees and regrew after Iniki. NTBG says its Kauai gardens reopened by 10-23. Held trees (Allerton figs, Poipu Road earpod, Hongwanji monkeypods): verdicts for the publish session in data/research/kauai-lowell-lifecheck-2026-11-03.md (the figs and monkeypods may go with the storm line, the earpod is the likeliest gone). A reader on Kauai would settle it.

**What yielded nothing:** Old Town and Presidio peppers (no single trunk placeable), the Huntington's Moreton Bay chestnut (no independent source). Rejects to data/leads/los-angeles.json and san-diego.json. About 320k tokens, 80k a tree; California is now near its yield floor for from-zero research.

Tested: preflight fails only on the pace (56 > 25); reverted. 31 trees is more than one day: the script takes `--only boston`.

The house-address register rows (Moreland St green ash champion, Lafayette St elm in Salem and others) were rejected as not shown to be visible from the street; under the El Pino ruling a street-level check could still ship them. Rejects to data/leads/boston.json, salem-massachusetts.json, concord-massachusetts.json, lexington-massachusetts.json. Six passes, about 976k tokens, 31k a tree.
