## 2026-11-03 session: two recovery gaps closed (the pace at the push, a release lane for every cut)

- **What changed in the product**: nothing a reader sees. Hidde read the Ahrefs and Search Console backlink reports (links come from press and the mails to nature organisations: Wilder, Baumkunde, Bomenstichting Den Haag, getLISBON; Reddit and Hacker News links carry nofollow) and asked whether we are doing everything to make the return go well and "geen fouten meer maken". Every switch stood in the safe position and 7,108 pages carry the Google noindex; two gaps were open and are closed:
- **The pace at the push**: `check_publishing_pace()` counted only while data/cities had uncommitted changes, so a session that committed first was never held. Main already carried 33 own trees in 24 hours (Pamplona's 11 with Hidde's one-time yes, the eight tourist places, Bali). `preflight.py --pace <remote>` now runs from the pre-push hook on HEAD against the base of 24 hours ago, and fails only when the push itself adds trees the remote lacks, so a burst already on main never stops a fix. The deploy still never fails on it.
- **The release lane** (thin_pages.py): lifting any cut (STRICT_CUT alone holds 2,279 pages) put the whole group back in one build, which qa's 80-url guard refuses, after which nothing deploys (the 10-09 lesson). Now at most 70 pages enter Google per deploy whatever the switches say: new pages first, then places and countries, English tree pages, translations, listings last, most-read cities first within a step; the rest keep the googlebot tag until a later build. Simulated with every switch off: 2,279 would enter, 70 went, all place pages. Listing pages (countries, species, parks, states) are now counted among the site's pages; the first simulation let 314 of them through uncounted. With the switches as they stand the lane is silent.
- **The weekly analysis** of 2026-11-02 failed in 343 ms before its first turn (a Claude action error, nothing of ours); dispatched again by hand.
- Rules in CLAUDE.md under Google recovery mode. The "role mandate" query in Search Console matches nothing on our pages.

**What changed (once built):** 12 trees from zero research (Hidde named the UK 2026-11-02), held in data/research/{canterbury,cambridge,oxford,stratford-upon-avon,mottisfont}-verified.json with place copy in data/research/uk-south-places.json, built by `python3 scripts/build_uk_south.py` (appends to live Oxford and Cambridge and grows their intro and meta; creates the new places whole), then `city_names.py`, preflight, commit by path. Canterbury (new, 4): the city's "baobab" planes in Westgate Gardens (Geograph photograph approved after a look, OSM-surveyed pin), at the castle, the cathedral precincts and Old Dover Road; every story says plainly that sources differ on the species (Oriental against the Natural History Museum's London Plane). Cambridge +4: the Botanic Garden's Henslow black pines, the Caucasian wingnut thicket, the 1949 dawn redwood and the champion chilgoza pine (one garden-centroid pin, a recognition line each). Oxford +1: Balliol's black mulberry, about 1609 by the college's claim. Stratford-upon-Avon (new, 2, tree pages only): the New Place mulberry believed grown from Shakespeare's tree, and Hall's Croft's (single 2013 source, flagged). Mottisfont (new, 1): the Great Plane, called the largest London plane in Britain. Tested: city_names.py fine, preflight fails only on the pace cap (45 in 24 h); files reverted.

**Nothing found:** Windsor 0 (Great Park oaks unplaceable or in private areas; the Conqueror's and Signing Oaks are leads for a Geograph tag search), Winchester 0 (no source sizes a single Close tree; six leads). Woodland Trust inventory untouched. Not reached: Cambridge colleges and Grantchester, Holy Trinity Stratford, Bath, York, Brighton (already live). Leads and blocked in data/leads/{windsor,winchester,canterbury,stratford-upon-avon,cambridge,oxford}.json.

**Cost:** about 768k tokens for 12 trees, about 64k a tree; Windsor (131k for nothing) and a session limit mid-pass were the waste. One write-pass Commons request carried Hidde's address in its user-agent header; later ones did not.

## 2026-11-03 session: southern Italy from MASAF, 45 trees written and held for the pace

- **Waiting, preflight-clean apart from the pace** (tested in a throwaway worktree, 78 own trees in 24 hours that night): Palermo +5 (27), Naples +7 (31), Caserta +4 (24), and new places Agrigento 8 (Valle dei Templi olives, Kolymbethra, Piazza Aldo Moro figs), Palazzolo Acreide 3 (the Villa Comunale cypress 7.2 m round, cedar, holm oak), Portici 5 (Reggia park), Castellammare di Stabia 3, Sila 3 (I Giganti della Sila, temporarily closed by FAI, said on the page; Gariglione fir and beech), Benevento 2, and one-tree places Noto, Ragusa (Donnafugata figs), Monte Faito (four beeches), San Pietro in Amantea (U Chiuppu plane), Serra San Bruno. Seven Commons photographs approved after a look (Agrigento's Juno olive, Noto's pine, the three Palazzolo trees, the Palermo ombu, the Sila grove); the Ucciardone plane's photo is held (trunk at the edge). Palermo's oldest tree is now the San Benedetto cypress (a 2021 core gave 444 years); the 1845 Orto fig stays the oldest fig. **To publish, once the pace allows:** `python3 scripts/build_italy_south.py` (also merges the Italian overlays for Naples and Palermo and fixes pal_019's two sentences that pal_024 makes false), then `city_names.py`, preflight, commit by path, push.
- **Held in the research files with a reason:** the Madonie sycamore (no route at 1612 m), Noto's Cava Candelaro carob and Santa Lucia oak (access), two Serra firs and two Aspromonte beeches (no source says which trunk). Leads: Catania's Scuola Superiore fig and Palermo's Palazzo Pantelleria fig (not public), Villa di Napoli (closed), the Celico chestnut (private), Sorrento's Via Rota trees (hotel grounds), Accademia di Belle Arti cloister. Modica and Caltagirone not reached.
- **Lessons:** OSM nodes tagged ref:masaf are register imports, not pin evidence; the Sicilian tourism GIS nodes and Esculapio's Wiki Loves Monuments 2026 geotags were the real pin sources. Campania's regional schede (static pages) are the second source there. Mid-run the photo-or-pin rule turned out to be off (findable.py): an approximate pin plus a recognition line ships.
- **Cost:** 4 verify passes (690k), 3 write passes (580k), 1 translation (128k), about 1.4M plus the dispatcher for 45 trees, about 35k a tree. My first builder test was done in place and blocked other sessions' pushes for a few minutes; tests now run in a worktree.

## 2026-11-03 session: central Italy from MASAF, 18 trees written and held for the pace (Spoleto and Vallombrosa new)

**What changed (once built):** 18 trees from the MASAF national register, held in data/research/{spoleto,vallombrosa}-verified.json and {rome,lucca,perugia,florence}-masaf-verified.json, built by `python3 scripts/build_italy_central.py` (writes the city files, merges the Italian overlays of Florence, Lucca and Rome; `--out DIR` for a dry run, `--only a,b` for a subset). New places: Spoleto (6: Villa Redenta cedar with a Commons photo, the Ippocastani redwood, the Palazzo Marignoli plane seen from Vicolo del Quartiere and pinned there, the Monteluco convent holm oak, two cypresses) and Vallombrosa (4: the arboretum's giant sequoia, confirmed by an iNaturalist GPS fix, the 51.5 m sugar pine, the Faggio Santo, the Faggione; a day trip from Florence). Live cities grow: Perugia 12 to 16 (Villa del Colle del Cardinale cedar with photo, its deodar and Monterey cypress, the Santa Giuliana holm oak), Lucca 14 to 16 (the Witches' Oak of Gragnano with photo, the Vinchiana cemetery cedar), Rome 31 to 32 (the Ostia Antica holm oak, Piazza Gregoriopoli), Florence 27 to 28 (the Via Pisana wisteria). Intros, FAQs and Italian overlays grow with them. Five Commons photos approved after viewing, the Faggio Santo photo held (which trunk). Tested: city_names.py fine, i18ncheck clean, preflight fails only on the pace cap; files reverted.

**What did not work:** Tuscany, Lazio and Umbria hold only about 440 unplaced MASAF rows, and Tivoli, Viterbo, Bomarzo, Siena, Chianti and Val d'Orcia have no usable single-tree rows. Most villa trees are private (Lucca's Matraia, Saltocchio, Vorno and San Gennaro villas; Palazzo Campello in Spoleto; Villa Colombella in Perugia; Belmonte and La Cipressa near Florence); Sammezzano is closed until 2028, Castelporziano is a closed estate, the San Sisto nursery opens a few days a year, the Villa Torlonia pine is unproven after the December 2025 felling. Rejects in data/leads/{spoleto,vallombrosa,rome,lucca,perugia,florence}.json. Overpass was down and Commons geosearch throttled all session, so no OSM pin checks. Liveness on the Spoleto recognition-line trees rests on undated regional sheets; said in their notes and flagged.

**Lesson:** this brief also asked for a photo or confirmed pin in Italy; that check is off while ADD_NEEDS_FINDABLE is False, and the passes first dropped 13 good trees for it. Cost about 1.15M tokens in agents (786k verify, 240k write, 123k translate), roughly 64k a tree.

**For a later session:** Florence's live FAQ answer on free visits says seven entries have no access note and "we would rather leave that blank than guess", which narrates our process; it predates this run.

New places: Sierra de Cazorla (5, one with an approved Commons photograph of the Pino de las Cruces), Sierra de Grazalema (3), Las Alpujarras (2) and Sierra de Aracena (1); the last three stay under four, so tree pages only. Held: the Ocuri wild olive (booked guided visits only; preflight reads that as needing permission, CLAUDE.md hard rule 10 says a booked tour counts as yes, so it waits on that contradiction) and the Zurraque oak (no path). **Lesson:** a brief asked for a photo or confirmed pin in Spain; that check is off while ADD_NEEDS_FINDABLE is False, and three public trees were rejected for it before the correction. Cost about 0.95M tokens plus a 154k translate pass, roughly 60k a tree.

## 2026-11-03 session: Tenerife, 14 trees written and held for the pace (Santa Cruz becomes its own place)

Two verify passes, one photo-judge pass, one write pass. Held in data/research/, nothing published: `python3 scripts/build_tenerife.py` writes santa-cruz-de-tenerife (sct_001 to sct_009, a walk of about four kilometres in the capital from the municipal register: Plaza del Príncipe laurel figs of 1864, Plaza de San Francisco Moreton Bay figs, García Sanabria kapok, tamarind and banyan, dragons at Plaza del Chicharro and the Concepción church, La Granja red gum and ombú), appends tfe_005 to tfe_009 to tenerife (Drago de San Antonio, Drago del Cristo in Tacoronte, Pino de Buen Paso, Pino del Señor in Arafo, the Hijuela del Botánico drago in La Orotava) with the place text rewritten for nine trees, and merges the five trees and new place text into the Spanish overlay (data/research/tenerife-es.json; a short overlay refuses the whole build). Four Commons photographs approved after viewing (sct_002, sct_008, tfe_005, tfe_006); four island pins confirmed; the rest ship on a recognition line, all with one. Tested: city_names.py and preflight clean apart from pace; test build reverted.
The Cabildo de Tenerife's singular-tree catalogue is scouted: about 300 trees, CC0 through the Cabildo's site-wide legal notice, one page per tree, no dataset to import (OPEN_DATA_SURVEY.md, data/register-scouting.json). About two thirds of its dragos are private; the Drago de San Francisco in Realejo Bajo died in March 2026. Not reached: La Laguna (its cathedral dragos are about 60 years old, one fell in August), Garachico, Jardín Victoria, Plaza del Charco. Overpass was down all session, so the Santa Cruz pins stay approximate. About 0.85M tokens, roughly 61k a tree.
For the session that publishes: run the builder once the queued 14:20 publish leaves room under the pace (14 trees), then city_names.py, preflight, commit per city.

## 2026-11-02 session: Greek islands, 10 trees written and held for the pace (Corfu 8, Kefalonia 1, Paros 1); a Greek tree register found

- **Waiting, written and preflight-clean apart from the pace (45 own trees in 24 hours when checked):** Corfu as a new island place of eight: the Moreton Bay figs (11.4 m round), the 31.5 m Aleppo pine and the Norfolk Island pine in Mon Repos, the Liston oak (declining, said so), the ombu of the People's Garden, the plane at Empress Elisabeth's spring in Gastouri (12.78 m measured on the wall built against it, 7.70 m free trunk, both said), the elm of Strinylas (age sources conflict, left open), and the gazette-declared olive of Agios Spyridon at Panachori (21.7 m round at the base; the 2,700 years is a press estimate, said so). Kefalonia: the Plane of Saint Gerasimos on the Omala plateau, about 450 years by ring count, gazette monument since 2024. Paros: the giant cypress (35 m) in the Valley of the Butterflies, ticketed June to September; the stone pine 22 m away folded into it. Pins are the registry's GPS points, confirmed, except the Panachori olive (gazette coordinate, approximate, with a location_site). No photographs: Commons rate-limited the geosearch all session, so `photo_hunt.py --recheck` is worth one try later. **To publish, once the pace allows:** `python3 scripts/build_greek_islands.py`, then `city_names.py`, preflight, commit by path. Stories in data/research/{corfu,kefalonia,paros}-verified.json, place fields in data/research/greek-islands-places.json.
- **Found: the Greek Tree Registry** of the Friends of Hellenic National Parks (filoiparkon.org/en/greek-tree-registry/<n>/): 200 field-recorded trees with GPS, girth, height, access and gazette status. © and not open, so facts and coordinates only, cited per entry. Facts-only crawl in data/research/greek-tree-registry-filoiparkon.json. Beyond the islands it holds 39 Athens trees, 17 Ioannina, 15 Evrytania, 14 Arcadia, 12 Pelion: the cheapest Greek supply there is.
- **Empty:** Rhodes (web sweep, no named pinnable tree; leads in data/leads/rhodes.json) and Kos beyond the Hippocrates plane (walled 48 h). The registry has nothing on either. Lesbos: one registry oak on private land, to leads.
- **Cost:** 5 verify passes (542k) and one writing pass (146k), about 690k for 10 trees, about 69k a tree. The web-only passes found 1 tree in 318k; the registry-backed ones found 9 in 224k plus the writing.

## 2026-11-02 session: Puglia second pass, 4 trees written and held for the pace (Pino di Lenne, Vico del Gargano's Capuchin holm oak, the Faggio dei Baracconi, the Bisceglie pistachio)

- **Correction, same evening:** the covered-country photo-or-confirmed-pin rule is off (`ADD_NEEDS_FINDABLE = False`); what binds is `check_a_tree_can_be_told_apart()`, which a recognition line satisfies. So two held trees came back in, register pins approximate with a `how_to_recognise` line: Foresta Umbra, the Faggio dei Baracconi (5.2 m, 26 m, one of five Puglia trees in the ministry's Alberi monumentali d'Italia; not dated alive recently, the story says so and asks readers), and Bisceglie, the pistachio of the Giardino Botanico Veneziani (2.68 m round, booked visits only, access says so). The builder now makes four places; preflight dry run with all four in place failed only on the pace (39 in 24 hours). The Tremiti ombu stays out on the selection floor, not the photo: an unmeasured registered group, and the iNaturalist photo shows a modest tree beside a shed.

- **Waiting, written and preflight-clean apart from the pace (37 own trees in 24 hours when checked):** two new places of one tree each. Palagiano, the Pino di Lenne, an Aleppo pine 4.45 m round in the Marina di Lenne pinewood (Stornara state reserve), which a national ISPRA dune report calls over 600 years old and, for some, Europe's oldest Aleppo pine (stated as the report's, no basis given); confirmed pin from an iNaturalist GPS fix labelled with the tree's name. Vico del Gargano, the holm oak of the Capuchins, 5.2 m round, planted by a friar some 300 years ago by its plaque, in the open friary forecourt; confirmed pin from an iNaturalist 4 m GPS fix whose photos I looked at (the oak with its ring bench). Neither has an open-licence photo (the only ones are CC BY-NC), so both stay out of Google until a reader adds one. **To publish, once the pace allows:** `python3 scripts/build_puglia_2.py`, then `city_names.py` (it proposes "Vieste" as an alias of Vico del Gargano: delete that one, Vieste is another town), preflight, commit by path. Stories in data/research/{palagiano,vico-del-gargano}-verified.json, place fields in data/research/puglia-2-held-2026-11-02.json. No overlap with the first pass's places (Lecce, Vernole, Martina Franca).
- **Kept in research, not shipped:** the Tremiti ombu on San Nicola (tmt_001, see the correction above). The Baracconi beech's one Commons photo, "Un faggio monumentale della foresta umbra.jpg", names no trunk or place, so it was not used.
- **Blocked:** the Foresta Umbra Falascone yews (the register's best cluster: eight yews within 100 m) stand in the fenced strict reserve, research visits only. Villa Sbisà in Bari is university property. Calena abbey in Peschici is closed. Rejects and register-only rows (Rodi Garganico's Piazza Garibaldi pine, San Menaio pines, Molfetta, the Polignano La Grave carobs, Foggia Incoronata oaks, Terlizzi) in data/leads/. Overpass was down all session, so no OSM tree nodes were checked; Commons geosearch found nothing within 150 m of ten register points.
- **Puglia verdict after two passes:** the register is mostly farmland, masserie and fenced reserves with no web footprint; about 7 trees from 1.2M tokens. Do not run it again without a new angle (a reader with GPS, the Gargano park's own trail booklet, or Overpass back for the Rodi pine and Foresta Umbra trail trees).
- **Cost:** 3 verify passes (416k) plus the dispatcher's own writing (about 110k, estimated): about 530k for 4 trees, about 130k a tree.

## 2026-11-02 session: Puglia, 3 trees written and held for the pace; the register is mostly masserie

- **Waiting, written and preflight-clean apart from the pace (38 in 24 hours when checked):** three new places of one tree each. Lecce, the ombu of Viale Marconi (about 150 years by the local press, confirmed pin from iNaturalist GPS fixes, no photo). Vernole, the Lizza dei Briganti holm oak at Pisignano (4.15 m round, 23 m, Puglia register; seen from the lane, on farmland). Martina Franca, the Albero del Capitano in the Bosco delle Pianelle reserve (free signed paths). The last two ship on Commons photographs (CC BY-SA 4.0) I looked at and approved; the Capitano's uploader names the tree. Trees in data/research/{lecce,vernole,martina-franca}-verified.json, place fields in data/research/puglia-held-2026-11-02.json. **The session that publishes them** builds data/cities/<slug>.json from those two files (same shape as gran-canaria.json; a builder sits on Hidde's Mac at out/tmp/puglia_build.py), then `city_names.py`, preflight, commit, push. They queue behind the trees already held for 11-03.
- **Why so few:** four verify passes (677k tokens) over Lecce, Otranto, Martina Franca, Alberobello, Ostuni with the Fasano olive plain, and Brindisi. Nearly every register row in these towns stands in a private masseria or field, and Italy needs a photograph or a confirmed pin, which the rest lack. Held in research or leads, ready the moment a photo or GPS fix arrives: Lequile's two ombu in Piazza Europa (girths 11 and 10.2 m, data/leads/lecce.json), the Brindisi Di Summa figs (brd_001 in data/research/brindisi-verified.json, in an active ASL health complex courtyard, access unconfirmed: do not publish as is), the Ostuni San Benedetto cork oak (5.6 m). Otranto, Alberobello and Fasano have no named public tree.
- **Puglia's monumental olives are not open data:** the L.R. 14/2007 list sits in a SIT web app; the regional library's items have survey photos but no coordinates and only cadastral codes, i.e. private land. The olive plain needs a walk route with a GPX track (the "Itinerario dei Giganti" along the Via Traiana) or readers, not a register pass. Do not re-run these towns without a new angle; the Gargano (Monte Sant'Angelo, Vico del Gargano, Foresta Umbra, about 60 register rows) is the untried Puglia supply.

Hidde: on the web map, searching Greece sent you to the Greece page instead of moving the map; and from the home search the map is more interesting than the country landing page (Airbnb does both). Now on /explore a country or city picked in the search frames the map on it and the list beside it follows (`window.atMapPlace` in site/src/lib/map.ts); in every other search field a country opens `/explore#country=<name>`. A city row elsewhere still opens the city page (itself a map with the walk, and where Google traffic lands). The app already did this. Tested against the live page through a local proxy carrying the patch (desktop and 375px); not yet seen after the CI build. CONVENTIONS.md "A place picked in the search lands on the map".

## 2026-11-01 write pass: New York +1, Dublin +1

Live: nyc_030 The Weeping Beech of Douglaston Station (traffic island opposite the LIRR station, by the WWI memorial; age disputed 1850 to 1880s, the Flushing-cutting story kept as belief; species name aligned to the canonical Weeping Beech) and dub_019 The Marlay Park Cedar of Lebanon (register-only, 2009 survey band 150 to 200 years, a tree-level grid pin rather than a park centre, alive unconfirmed and said so on the page). Count promises moved: Dublin 19, New York 29. Preflight 0 problems.
Held, reasons on the rows: nyc_031 Saratoga red oak (only a park-centre position in a full city block, so nobody could find the trunk), tav_001 (resort access unproven), frk_027 (schoolyard, no viewing place sourced), inn_007 (pin on a building, access unsettled), sfo_012 (duplicate of live sfo_004). The six READY leads were all declined again and now carry a `write_pass_2026_11_01` hold note so `leads.py --ready` stops offering them: the three Paris rows (Montsouris low branch: no species, no trunk; Bercy 1895 plane: no coordinate, island is par_042; Talus plane: planted 1935), the two Oxford rows (Headington oak: one source, park-centre pin; Iffley chestnut: no life evidence since about 2008) and the Baarn beech (our own photograph, not a reader's, no note, which trunk still open).

## 2026-11-01 01:30 window (night run)

Rungs 1 and 2 clear (no reader photos or notes, health green). Portugal's cities are all on the 48h wall, so I took the six verified-but-unwritten trees. Product: Dublin +1, the Marlay Park Yew (dub_018, no photo, approximate pin, a recognition line carries it). Held with reasons: dub_019 (no basis to tell it apart), tav_001 (Pedras d'el Rei resort, access unproven), frk_027 (schoolyard), inn_007 (pin on a building, access unsettled), sfo_012 (duplicate of sfo_004, its DataSF pin and 176 cm girth belong in an enrichment of sfo_004). Enrichment of paris+madrid+frankfurt: only par_071 got a best_time (fruit, September); 11 other gaps went to dead ends for 90 days (pins equal their register coordinates; frankfurt.de sits behind a Cloudflare challenge, so none of its city pages could be opened; mad_018 was not searched). Then the two RETRY scouts in Portugal, both still empty. Coimbra: cm-coimbra.pt answers through the r.jina.ai proxy (the 403 only hits direct fetches), Anexo VII lists seven trees and every one is already live or already a lead (three are on private quintas or a village street). Lagos: ICNF Algarve rows reach no four trees (no life evidence or access); Monchique's three are 1.5 h away. Worth knowing: `passcheck.py --brief lagos` searches Lagos, Nigeria, so its zero candidates tell us nothing about Portugal. Faro (eastern Algarve) and Savannah scouts also retried: no cluster of four in Faro district (tav_001 stays held on resort access); Savannah can only move on a permission ask to the Georgia Tree Council, which is Hidde's call. Leftover Algarve source for a later pass: Almargem's 2007 Loule inventory (78 trees with GPS, image-only PDF, needs OCR). New York verify (US supply): 89 open leads, only two shippable and thin: nyc_030 Douglaston weeping beech (two sources, 30 m site, no photo) and nyc_031 Saratoga red oak (one source, park-level pin, still unfindable). NJDEP rows are house addresses (rule 10), Westchester rows carry no metadata, nycgovparks.org is blocked even through r.jina.ai. The new-york claim stays in place until a writer takes nyc_030. Breakage: none. For Hidde: nothing.
